Define an initial pasted submeasurement as follows:

H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}=G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}.

This forms a submeasurement because of each of the individual
G^{x}
’s are
submeasurements.
Then, for all
h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
, define the following operators

H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{h}=H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{h|_{x_{1}},\ldots,h|_{x_{t}}}

where
h|_{x_{j}}
denotes the codeword in
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}
that comes from
setting the
(m+1)
-st coordinate of
h
to
x_{j}
.
This set
H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}=\{H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{h}\}
forms a submeasurement.
This is because the set

S=\{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{t})\in{\left(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes{m}}\right)}^{t}:\text{there exists $h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}$ such that }h|_{x_{j}}=g_{j}\}

is a subset of
{\left(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes{m}}\right)}^{t}
, and furthermore
S
is in one-to-one correspondence
with
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
as shown in
Proposition

2.19
.
Thus

\sum_{h}H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{h}=\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{t})\in S}H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\leq\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{t})\in{\left(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes{m}}\right)}^{t}}H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\leq\mathbf{1}.

And finally, define the operators

H_{h}=\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],t)}H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}_{h}

where we define
\mathsf{distinct}([n],t)
to denote the set of tuples

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in{[n]}^{t}
such that all of the coordinates are distinct.
It is easy to verify that
H=\{H_{h}\}
forms a submeasurement.
In what follows,
H
will interchangeably refer to both the set
\{H_{h}\}
as
well as the sum
\sum_{h}H_{h}
; it should be clear from context which we are
referring to. We will repeatedly use the following simple claim.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
n,k\geq 1
be integer.
Let
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\sim[n]^{k}
be sampled uniformly at random
and let
y=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)
.
Then

d_{\mathrm{TV}}(x,y)\leq\frac{k^{2}}{n}.

Proof.

For any
z=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k})\in[n]^{k}
,

\displaystyle\Pr(x=z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q^{k}},

\displaystyle\Pr(y=z)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\frac{1}{\binom{n}{k}k!}&\text{if $z\in\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Because
\frac{1}{\binom{n}{k}k!}\geq\frac{1}{n^{k}}
,
\Pr(x=z)\geq\Pr(y=z)
if and only if
z\notin\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)
.
Hence,

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{TV}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\max_{S\subseteq[n]^{k}}\{\Pr(x\in S)-\Pr(y\in S)\}

\displaystyle=\Pr(x\in\overline{\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)})-\Pr(y\in\overline{\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)})=\Pr(x\in\overline{\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}]\;.

We can upper-bound this probability as follows.

\displaystyle\Pr(x\in\overline{\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)})

\displaystyle=\Pr(\exists i:x_{i}\in\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1}\})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=2}^{k}\Pr(x_{i}\in\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1}\})\leq\sum_{i=2}^{k}\left(\frac{i-1}{n}\right)=\frac{k(k-1)}{2n}\;.

∎

6.3.1
Bounding the consistency of
H
with the
A
measurements

First we bound the consistency of
H
with line measurements
B^{\ell}
where

\ell
are parallel to the
(m+1)
-st axis.
In other words, we consider lines
\ell
consisting of points that only vary in
the
(m+1)
-st coordinate.
Since we focus on such lines, we use the following abbreviation:

B^{u}_{f}=B^{\ell}_{f}

where
\ell=\ell(m+1,u)
(see
Definition

2.16
for the notation for axis-parallel lines).

Since
(\tau,A,B,P)
is an
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-good strategy for

\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
, we have by definition that

\E_{\ell,y\sim\ell}\sum_{f\in\mathcal{C}}\tau\left(B^{\ell}_{f}\,A^{y}_{f(y_{j})}\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon

where the expectation is over all axis-parallel lines

\ell=\ell(j,\alpha_{-j})
(not necessarily the ones parallel to direction

m+1
) and points
y\in\ell
.
Since a line
\ell
that is parallel to the
(m+1)
-st direction is selected
with probability
1/(m+1)
, this implies that

\E_{(u,x)\in{[n]}^{m}\times[n]}\sum_{f\in\mathcal{C}}\tau\left(B^{u}_{f}\,A^{u,x}_{[a:a\neq f(x)]}\right)\leq(m+1)\varepsilon\;.

(84)

Define measurement
\{B^{u,x}_{a}\}
as

B^{u,x}_{a}=B^{u}_{[f\mapsto f(x)\mid a]}\;.

Then
Eq.

84
can be also written as

\E_{(u,x)\in{[n]}^{m}\times[n]}\sum_{a,b:a\neq b}\tau\left(B^{u,x}_{b}A^{u,x}_{a}\right)\leq(m+1)\varepsilon\;.

(85)

By
Proposition

2.5
this implies that on average over

(u,x)\in{[n]}^{m}\times[n]
,

B^{u,x}_{a}\approx_{\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}}A^{u,x}_{a}\;.

(86)

We first show that the
\{H_{h}\}
submeasurement and the
\{B^{u}_{f}\}

measurements are consistent.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
\nu_{3}=t\cdot\left(t\cdot\nu_{2}+\sqrt{\zeta+\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}}\right)
.
On average over
u
sampled uniformly from
{[n]}^{m}
, we have

H_{[h\mapsto h|_{u}\mid f]}\simeq_{\nu_{3}}B^{u}_{f}

where the answer summation is over
f\in\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be the uniform distribution on
\mathsf{distinct}([n],t)
and
\mathcal{D}_{i}
be the marginal
distribution of
\mathcal{D}
on
i
coordinates.

\displaystyle\E_{u}\sum_{f^{\prime}\neq f}\tau\left(H_{[h\mapsto h|_{u}\mid f^{\prime}]}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{u}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)},f\in\mathcal{C}\\
h|_{u}\neq f\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H_{h}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)},f\in\mathcal{C}:\\
h|_{u}\neq f\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H_{h}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,f:\\
\exists\,i:h(u,x_{i})\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H_{h}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,f:\\
h(u,x_{i})\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H_{h}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)

For any fixed
i\in[t]
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,f:\\
h(u,x_{i})\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H_{h}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,f:\\
h(u,x_{i})\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{h|_{x_{1}}}\cdots\,G^{x_{t}}_{h|_{x_{t}}}\cdots\,G^{x_{1}}_{h|_{x_{1}}}\cdot B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1},\ldots,g_{t},f:\\
g_{i}(u)\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdot B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i})\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}}\tau\left(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\cdot G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot\Bigl(G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot G^{x_{i-1}}_{g_{i-1}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\Bigr)\right)

\displaystyle\approx_{i\cdot\nu_{2}\;}

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i})\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}}\tau\left(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\cdot G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot\Bigl(G^{x_{i-1}}_{g_{i-1}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdot G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\Bigr)\right)

(87)

where the inequality comes from the fact that
G^{x_{i+1}},\ldots,G^{x_{t}}
are submeasurements, and the last approximation follows from

Propositions

2.7
and
2.11
and the approximate
commutativity of the
G^{x}
measurements (
Lemma

6.3
).
Continuing on, we can use Cauchy-Schwarz to bound (
87
) by

(
87
)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i})\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\sqrt{\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i})\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{i-1}}_{g_{i-1}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdot G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot{\bigl(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\bigr)}^{2}\cdot G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1}\cdot\sqrt{\E_{u,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{i}}\tau\left(G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\,B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\right)}

where in the last inequality we used the fact that the
G^{x}
is projective
and

{\bigl(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\bigr)}^{2}\leq B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}

for all
u
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\E_{u,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{i}}\tau\left(G^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{u,x}\sum_{a,b:a\neq b}\tau\left(G^{u,x}_{a}B^{u,x}_{b}\right)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}}\E_{u,x}\sum_{a,b:a\neq b}\tau\left(G^{u,x}_{a}A^{u,x}_{b}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\zeta\;.

where the approximation in the second line follows from
Proposition

2.7
and
Eq.

86
.
Putting everything together, this implies that

\E_{u}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)},f\in\mathcal{C}\\
h|_{u}\neq f\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H_{h}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)\leq\nu_{3},

for
\nu_{3}
given in the statement of the lemma.
∎

Next we argue that
H
is consistent with the points measurements
A
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\nu_{4}=\nu_{3}+\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}
. On average over
(u,x)
sampled uniformly from
{[n]}^{m}\times[n]
, we have

H_{[h\mapsto h(u,x)\mid a]}\simeq_{\nu_{4}}A^{u,x}_{a}\;.

where the answer summation is over
a\in\Sigma
.

Proof.

Define
H^{u,x}_{a}=H_{[h\mapsto h(u,x)|a]}
. We have

\begin{split}\E_{u,x}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b:\\
a\neq b\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H^{u,x}_{a}B^{u,x}_{b}\right)&=\E_{u,x}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b:\\
a\neq b\end{subarray}}\tau\left(\biggl(\sum_{h:h(u,x)=a}H_{h}\biggr)\biggl(\sum_{f:f(x)=b}B^{u}_{f}\biggr)\right)\\
&=\E_{u,x}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,f:\\
h(u,x)\neq f(x)\end{subarray}}\tau\left(H_{h}B^{u}_{f}\right)\\
&\leq\E_{u,x}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,f\end{subarray}:h|_{u}\neq f}\tau\left(H_{h}B^{u}_{f}\right),\end{split}

which is at most
\nu_{3}
by
Lemma

6.5
. That is

H^{u,x}_{a}\simeq_{\nu_{3}}B^{u,x}_{a}.

Applying
Proposition

2.9
to the above and
Eq.

86

proves that

H^{u,x}_{a}\simeq_{\nu_{4}}A^{u,x}_{a},

for
\nu_{4}=\nu_{3}+\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}
.

∎

6.3.2
Bounding the completeness of
H

We now bound the completeness of
H
. Let
G=\E_{x}G^{x}
where
G^{x}=\sum_{g}G^{x}_{g}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\nu_{5}=\sqrt{\nu_{4}}+\sqrt{\zeta}
. Then

\tau(H)\approx_{\nu_{5}}\tau(HG)~.

Proof.

We bound the difference

\displaystyle\tau\left(H(\mathbf{1}-G)\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{x}\sum_{h}\tau\left(H_{h}(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})\right)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\nu_{4}}\;\;}\E_{u,x}\sum_{h}\tau\left(H_{h}A^{u,x}_{h(u,x)}(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})\right).

(88)

To see the approximation in the second line, it suffices to bound the
difference as

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{u,x}\sum_{h}\tau\left(H_{h}\bigl(\mathbf{1}-A^{u,x}_{h(u,x)}\bigr)(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\;\;

\displaystyle\sqrt{\E_{u,x}\sum_{h}\tau\left(H_{h}\bigl(\mathbf{1}-A^{u,x}_{h(u,x)}\bigr)^{2}\right)}\cdot\sqrt{\E_{u,x}\sum_{h}\tau\left(H_{h}(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})^{2}\right)}

Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\E_{u,x}\sum_{a}\tau\left(H_{[h:h(u,x)\neq a]}A^{u,x}_{a}\right)}\cdot\sqrt{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\nu_{4}}

where the third line follows from the projectivity of the points measurements,
G^{x}\leq\mathbf{1}
, and
\{H_{h}\}
is a submeasurement.
Continuing the proof, we bound the absolute value of
Eq.

88
as

\displaystyle\left|\E_{u,x}\sum_{h}\tau\left(H_{h}A^{u,x}_{h(u,x)}(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;\;

\displaystyle\sqrt{\E_{u,x}\sum_{h}\tau\left(H_{h}\right)}\cdot\sqrt{\E_{u,x}\sum_{h}\tau\left((\mathbf{1}-G^{x}){\bigl(A^{u,x}_{h(u,x)}\bigr)}^{2}(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})H_{h}\right)}

Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{1}\cdot\sqrt{\E_{x}\sum_{h}\tau\left((\mathbf{1}-G^{x})H_{h}(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})\E_{u}A^{u,x}_{h(u,x)}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\E_{x}\sum_{h}\psi^{x}((\mathbf{1}-G^{x})\,H_{h}\,(\mathbf{1}-G^{x}))}

Assumption
3
of
Lemma

4.3

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\E_{x}\psi^{x}(\mathbf{1}-G^{x})}

\psi^{x}
is positive

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\zeta}

\displaystyle\text{Assumption~\ref{enu:pasting-boundedness} of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:pasting}}.

∎

We now argue that
HG
can be approximated by
G^{t+1}
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\nu_{6}=2t^{2}(\nu_{2}+n^{-1})
. Then

\tau(H)\approx_{\nu_{6}}\tau(G^{t})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tau(HG)\approx_{\nu_{6}}\tau(G^{t+1})~.

Proof.

We prove the first approximation.

\displaystyle\tau\left(H\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],t)}\sum_{h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{h|_{x_{1}}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{h|_{x_{t}}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{h|_{x_{1}}}\right)

(89)

\displaystyle=\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],t)}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\right)

(90)

\displaystyle\approx_{t^{2}/n}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim[n]^{t}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\right),

(91)

where in going from the first to the second line we used

Proposition

2.20
(which uses the interpolability of
\mathcal{C}
) to rewrite the sum over
h
to
sums over
t
-tuples of polynomials
g_{1},\dots,g_{t}
; and in
going from the second to the third line, we used
Proposition

6.4
and that the absolute value of

\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\cdots G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\right)

for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}
is at most
1
.
Continuing, we have

(
91
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim[n]^{t}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\cdots G^{x_{2}}_{g_{2}}\right)

Cyclicity of the trace

\displaystyle\approx_{(2t-5)\nu_{2}\;}

\displaystyle\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim[n]^{t}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\cdots G^{x_{3}}_{g_{3}}\right)

In other words, the right-most
G^{x_{2}}_{g_{2}}
has been commuted leftwards to where the
left occurence of
G^{x_{2}}_{g_{2}}
is; this requires
2t-5
(approximate)
commutations.
This follows from
Proposition

2.11
, the approximate commutativity of the

G
’s, and the projectivity of the
G
’s.
We can then commute the right-hand
G^{x_{3}}_{g_{3}}
to the left
2t-7
places,
and so on.
Adding up all the errors together, we get that

\tau(H)\approx_{t^{2}(\nu_{2}+n^{-1})}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\sim[n]^{t}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\tau\left(G^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots G^{x_{t}}_{g_{t}}\right)=\tau\left(G^{t}\right)

where we used that
\sum_{j=2}^{t-1}(2(t-j)-1)\leq t^{2}
.

The second approximation in the Lemma statement follows from a nearly identical
argument.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\nu_{7}=\nu_{6}+2(\nu_{5}+2\nu_{6})^{1/t}
. Then

\tau(H)\geq\tau(G)-\nu_{7}~.

Proof.

Since for all real numbers
0\leq\lambda\leq 1
and integer
t\geq 2
, we have

\lambda(1-\lambda^{t-1})\leq 2\Big(\lambda^{t}(1-\lambda)\Big)^{1/t}

we have the operator inequality

G-G^{t}\leq 2\Big(G^{t}(\mathbf{1}-G)\Big)^{1/t}\;.

and thus applying
\tau\left(\cdot\right)
on both sides, we have

\tau(G-G^{t})\leq 2\tau\left(\Big(G^{t}(\mathbf{1}-G)\Big)^{1/t}\right)\leq 2{\Bigl(\tau\left(G^{t}-G^{t+1}\right)\Bigr)}^{1/t}

where we used that
\tau\left(X^{1/t}\right)\leq\tau\left(X\right)^{1/t}
for all positive operators
X
(this is a consequence of Hölder’s inequality, see
[
PX03
]
). This implies that

|\tau(H)-\tau(G)|\leq\tau(G-G^{t})+|\tau(H-G^{t})|\leq\tau(G-G^{t})+\nu_{6}

and

\tau(G-G^{t})\leq 2\Big(|\tau\left(G^{t}-H\right)|+|\tau\left(H-HG\right)|+\left|\tau\left(HG-G^{t+1}\right)\right|\Big)^{1/t}\leq 2(\nu_{6}+\nu_{5}+\nu_{6})^{1/t}

where we used
Lemmas

6.7
and
6.8
.
Put altogether we have

|\tau(H)-\tau(G)|\leq\nu_{6}+2(\nu_{5}+2\nu_{6})^{1/t}.

∎

6.3.3
Putting everything together

We now finish the proof of
Lemma

4.3
, with the error function
\mu
specified in (
83
) instead of (
5
). (The claimed bound (
5
) is shown in the next section.) Let
H^{\prime}=\{H^{\prime}_{h}\}
denote the completion of
H=\{H_{h}\}
as follows: letting
h^{*}
be the lexicographically first element of
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
, we set
H^{\prime}_{h^{*}}=H_{h^{*}}+(\mathbf{1}-H)
. Then for all
h\neq h^{*}
, define
H_{h}^{\prime}=H_{h}
. By construction
H^{\prime}
is a perfectly complete measurement. We now evaluate its consistency with
A
:

\displaystyle\E_{u,x}\sum_{h,a:h(u,x)\neq a}\tau\left(H^{\prime}_{h}\,A^{u,x}_{a}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\E_{u,x}\sum_{h,a:h(u,x)\neq a}\tau\left(H_{h}\,A^{u,x}_{a}\right)+\sum_{a}\tau\left((\mathbf{1}-H)A^{u,x}_{a}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{u,x}\sum_{h,a:h(u,x)\neq a}\tau\left(H_{h}\,A^{u,x}_{a}\right)+\tau\left((\mathbf{1}-H)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{4}+(1-\tau\left(G\right))+\nu_{7}\;.

Letting
\mu=\kappa+\nu_{4}+\nu_{7}
where
\kappa=\tau(G)
, we get that
H^{\prime}
satisfies the conclusions stated in
Lemma

4.3
, once we verify that
\mu
has the correct asymptotic dependence on
m,t,\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n,t
.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\varepsilon\leq 1/(m+1)
,
\delta\leq 1
,

\zeta\leq 1
as otherwise the lemma is trivial.
Recall also that by Proposition
2.18
,
\gamma_{m}\leq(mt)/n
.
Under these conditions, we have

\displaystyle\nu_{1}

\displaystyle=8(\sqrt{\zeta}+\sqrt{(m+1)\delta})=\mathrm{poly}(m)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\delta,\zeta)

\displaystyle\nu_{2}

\displaystyle=4(\gamma_{m}+\nu_{1})=\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\mathrm{poly}\left(\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\nu_{3}

\displaystyle=t\left(t\nu_{2}+\sqrt{\zeta+\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}}\right)=\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\nu_{4}

\displaystyle=\nu_{3}+\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}=\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\nu_{5}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\nu_{4}}+\sqrt{\zeta}=\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\nu_{6}

\displaystyle=2t^{2}\left(v_{2}+n^{-1}\right)=\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\nu_{7}

\displaystyle=\nu_{6}+2{(\nu_{5}+2\nu_{6})}^{1/t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)+\left(\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{t}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\left(\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{t}}.

Therefore,

\mu=\kappa+\mathrm{poly}(m,t)\cdot\left(\mathrm{poly}\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1}\right)\right)^{1/t}

as required.

6.4
Pasting the
G
’s together: Method 2

The second construction of
H=\{H_{h}\}

is designed to circumvent the problem of the first construction,
which is that its soundness has a rather poor dependence on the parameter
t
.
Before describing the construction, we need the following definitions.

Definition 6.10

(
G
’s incomplete part)
.

For each
x\in[n]
we write
G^{x}=\sum_{g}G^{x}_{g}
and
G^{x}_{\bot}=\mathbf{1}-G^{x}

for the “complete” and “incomplete” parts of
G^{x}
respectively.
Let
\widehat{G}=\{\widehat{G}^{x}_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}\cup\{\bot\}}

be the projective measurement defined as

\widehat{G}^{x}_{g}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}G^{x}_{g}&\text{if }g\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m},\\
G^{x}_{\bot}&\text{if }g=\bot.\end{array}\right.

For succinctness we denote
\mathcal{C}^{+}=\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}\cup\{\bot\}
, leaving the parameter
m
implicit.

Definition 6.11

(Types)
.

A type
\tau
is an element of
\{0,1\}^{k}
for some integer
k
.
We write
|\tau|=\tau_{1}+\cdots+\tau_{k}
for the Hamming weight of
\tau
.
We often associate
\tau
with the set
\{i:\tau_{i}=1\}
and write
i\in\tau
if
\tau_{i}=1
.

Suppose that the measurement
\widehat{G}
is performed some
k\geq 1
times in succession,
generating the outcomes
g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}
.
Let us write
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{k}
for the “type” of these outcomes, where

\tau_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}1&\text{if $g_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}$}\;,\\
0&\text{if $g_{i}=\bot$\;.}\end{array}\right.

Whenever
\tau\geq t
, assuming that the
g_{i}
are not inconsistent
using Proposition
2.19
we can interpolate them to produce an
h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
.
Hence, we would like to understand the probability that
|\tau|\geq t

and ensure that it is as large as possible.
The probability that
\widehat{G}
returns a
g\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}

is equal to the completeness of
G
, which is
1-\kappa
.
This tells us that the probability that
\tau_{1}=1
is
1-\kappa
.
We might naively expect that the same holds for the other
\tau_{i}
as well.
We might also naively expect that the
\tau_{i}
are independent.
These two assumptions should not be expected to hold in general,
as they ignore correlations between the measurements.
However, if we make these assumptions, then we at least have a simple toy model
for the measurement outcomes:
\tau\sim\mathrm{Binomial}(k,1-\kappa)
.

In this toy model,
we expect
|\tau|\approx k\cdot(1-\kappa)
on average.
This was the limitation of the first construction:
if
k=t
and
\kappa
is reasonably large (say, on the order of
1/t
),
then we don’t expect
|\tau|
to be larger than
t
with high probability,
and so we can’t interpolate to produce a global codeword.
This suggests an alternative strategy:
choose
k
large enough so that
k\cdot(1-\kappa)\gg t
.
In fact, as we are aiming for
H
to have completeness close to
1-\kappa
,
we should choose
k
so large that
|\tau|\geq t
with probability
roughly
1-\kappa
.
On the other hand, if we set
k

too
large,
then we increase the risk that the
k
outcomes
g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}
are inconsistent with each other,
which is an additional source of error.

This “naive analysis” motivates our second construction of
H
,
which is stated below.
We will show that the naive analysis,
in which we treat
\tau
as a binomial random variable
and bound
|\tau|
using a Chernoff bound,
can be formalized.

Definition 6.12

(The pasted measurement)
.

Let
k\geq t
be an integer.

1.

(Pasting):
Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in[n]
.
Define initial “sandwiched” measurement operators as follows:

\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\,=\,\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{2}}_{g_{2}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{k}}_{g_{k}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{2}}_{g_{2}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\;.

2.

(Interpolation):
Let
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)
.
For any
w\in\{0,1\}^{k}
and
h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
,
define
h_{w}=(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in(\mathcal{C}^{+})^{k}

as
g_{i}=\bot
if
w_{i}=0
and
g_{i}=h|_{x_{i}}
otherwise.
Define the interpolated measurement

H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{h}\,=\,\sum_{w:|w|\geq t}\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{h_{w}}\;.

3.

(Averaging):
Define

H_{h}\,=\,\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{h}\;.

To analyze this construction,
we first need to show that
\widehat{G}
commutes with itself.
This is shown in
Section

6.4.1
,
using that a similar property holds for
G
.
Using this in
Section

6.4.2
we prove that
\widehat{H}
is consistent with
B
,
which we use to prove that
H
is consistent with
A
.
Finally, we analyze the completeness of
H
in
Section

6.4.3
.

6.4.1
Commutativity of
\widehat{G}

In this section we show that
\widehat{G}
commutes with itself.
As we already know this holds for the sub-measurement
G
,
our task essentially reduces to showing that this holds for
G
’s incomplete part,
i.e.
G_{\perp}
.
As it is more convenient to work with
G=\mathbf{1}-G_{\perp}
rather than
G_{\perp}
,
we will first show that these properties hold for
G
;
the fact that they also hold for
G_{\perp}
will then follow as an immediate corollary.

Lemma 6.13

(Commutativity with
G_{g}^{x}
implies commutativity with
G^{x}
)
.

For all
y\in[n]
let
M^{y}=\{M^{y}_{o}\}
be a projective sub-measurement with outcomes in some set
\mathcal{O}
and let
\chi>0
.
Suppose that

G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}\,\approx_{\chi}\,M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g}\;,

(92)

on average over independent and uniformly random
x,y\in[n]
. Then

G^{x}M^{y}_{o}\,\approx_{\sqrt{3\chi}}\,M^{y}_{o}G^{x}\;.

Proof.

We first show that

\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}G^{x}M^{y}_{o}\big)\,\approx_{{\chi}}\,\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}\big)

(93)

(note that on the left hand side, the third factor is
G^{x}
instead of
G^{x}_{g}
). To show this, we take the difference to obtain

\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\Big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}\big(G^{x}-G^{x}_{g}\big)M^{y}_{o}\Big)=\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\Big(G^{x}_{g}G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}\big(G^{x}-G^{x}_{g}\big)M^{y}_{o}\Big)

(94)

where we used the fact that
\{G^{x}_{g}\}
is projective. Notice that

\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g}(G^{x}-G^{x}_{g})M^{y}_{o}\big)\,=\,0

(95)

using again the fact that
\{G^{x}_{g}\}
is projective. Thus to prove (
93
) it suffices to show that the quantity in (
94
) is
\chi
-close to the quantity in (
95
). Taking the difference, we get

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}

\displaystyle\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}-M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g})(G^{x}-G^{x}_{g})M^{y}_{o}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\big((G^{x}-G^{x}_{g})M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g}(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}-M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g})\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\big((G^{x}-G^{x}_{g})M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}(G^{x}-G^{x}_{g})\big)\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\big((G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}-M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g})^{2}\big)\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq{\chi}\;,

(96)

where the second line follows from the cyclicity of the trace, the third line uses Cauchy-Schwarz and the fourth bounds the first term by
1
using
\|G^{x}-G^{x}_{g}\|\leq 1
for all
x,g
, cyclicity of the trace and
\{G^{x}\}
,
\{M^{y}\}
sub-measurements, and the second term by
\chi
using (
92
).

To prove the lemma we expand

\displaystyle\E_{x,y}\sum_{o}\tau\big((G^{x}M^{y}_{o}-M^{y}_{o}G^{x})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x,y}\sum_{o}\Big(2\,\tau\big(G^{x}M^{y}_{o}G^{x}M^{y}_{o}\big)-2\,\tau\big(G^{x}M^{y}_{o}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\Big(2\,\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}G^{x}M^{y}_{o}\big)-2\,\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\approx_{2\chi}\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\Big(2\,\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}\big)-2\,\tau\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,o}\tau\Big(\big(G^{x}_{g}M^{y}_{o}-M^{y}_{o}G^{x}_{g}\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\chi^{2}\;.

The third line follows from (
96
), and the last line follows again from (
92
).
∎

Corollary 6.14

(Commutativity of
G
’s)
.

Recall the error parameter
\nu_{2}
from
Lemma

6.3
. Then

\E_{x,y}\tau\big((G^{x}G^{y}-G^{x}G^{y})^{2}\big)\leq 9\nu_{2}^{1/4}~.

Proof.

We prove this via two applications of
Lemma

6.13
. First, by
Lemma

6.3
, we have that

G^{x}_{g}G^{y}_{h}\approx_{\nu_{2}}G^{y}_{h}G^{x}_{g}

on average of
x,y\sim[n]
. By
Lemma

6.13
, we get that

G^{x}G^{y}_{h}\approx_{3\sqrt{\nu_{2}}}G^{y}_{h}G^{x}~.

By applying
Lemma

6.13
again with the projective sub-measurement
M^{x}=\{G^{x}\}
with a single outcome, we get that

G^{y}G^{x}\approx_{9\nu_{2}^{1/4}}G^{x}G^{y}~.

∎

Corollary 6.15

(Commutativity of
\widehat{G}
)
.

Recall the error parameter
\nu_{2}
from
Lemma

6.3
. Let
\nu^{\prime}_{2}=27\nu_{2}^{1/4}
. Then

\displaystyle\widehat{G}^{x}_{g}\widehat{G}^{y}_{h}

\displaystyle\approx_{\nu^{\prime}_{2}}\widehat{G}^{y}_{h}\widehat{G}^{x}_{g}\;.

(97)

Proof.

We can write

\displaystyle\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}}\tau\big((\widehat{G}^{x}_{g}\widehat{G}^{y}_{h}-\widehat{G}^{y}_{h}\widehat{G}^{x}_{g})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=~\E_{x,y}\sum_{g,h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}}\tau\big((G^{x}_{g}G^{y}_{h}-G^{y}_{h}G^{x}_{g})^{2}\big)+\E_{x,y}\tau\big((G^{x}_{\bot}G^{y}_{\bot}-G^{y}_{\bot}G^{x}_{\bot})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\E_{x,y}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}}\tau\big((G^{x}_{g}G^{y}_{\bot}-G^{y}_{\bot}G^{x}_{g})^{2}\big)+\E_{x,y}\sum_{h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}}\tau\big((G^{x}_{\bot}G^{y}_{h}-G^{y}_{h}G^{x}_{\bot})^{2}\big)~.

(98)

We can bound the first term by
\nu_{2}^{2}
by
Lemma

6.3
. We can bound the second term by first writing

\displaystyle\E_{x,y}\tau\big((G^{x}_{\bot}G^{y}_{\bot}-G^{y}_{\bot}G^{x}_{\bot})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x,y}\tau\big(((\mathbf{1}-G^{x})(\mathbf{1}-G^{y})-(\mathbf{1}-G^{y})(\mathbf{1}-G^{x}))^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x,y}\tau\big((G^{x}G^{y}-G^{y}G^{x})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 9^{2}\cdot\nu_{2}^{1/2}

where the last line follows from
Corollary

6.14
. Similarly, we can bound the third and fourth terms of (
98
) by

\displaystyle\E_{x,y}\sum_{g}\tau\big((G^{x}_{g}G^{y}_{\bot}-G^{y}_{\bot}G^{x}_{g})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x,y}\sum_{g}\tau\big((G^{x}_{g}(\mathbf{1}-G^{y})-(\mathbf{1}-G^{y})G^{x}_{g})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x,y}\sum_{g}\tau\big((G^{x}_{g}G^{y}-G^{y}G^{x}_{g})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 9\nu_{2}

where the last line follows from
Lemma

6.3
and
Lemma

6.13
.

Putting everything together, this implies that we can upper bound (
98
) by
\nu_{2}^{2}+9^{2}\nu_{2}^{1/2}+18\nu_{2}\leq 3\cdot 9^{2}\cdot\nu_{2}^{1/2}
. The lemma follows.
∎

6.4.2
Consistency of
\widehat{H}
and
H
with
B
and
A

We use the same notation as in Section
6.3.1
. The following lemma is analogous to Lemma
6.5
.

Lemma 6.16

(Consistency of
\widehat{H}
with
B
)
.

Let
\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}=k\cdot\nu^{\prime}_{2}+(\zeta+(2(m+1)\varepsilon)^{1/2})^{1/2}
. For any
1\leq i\leq k
,

\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}}\sum_{a\neq\bot}\sum_{b\neq a}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}}_{[(g_{1},\dots,g_{k})\mapsto g_{i}(u)\mid a]}B^{u}_{[f\mapsto f(x_{i})\mid b]}\big)\,\leq\,\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}

(99)

where the expectation over
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})
is over independently random
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\sim[n]
.

Proof.

The proof follows closely that of Lemma
6.5
.
We have

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{g_{1},\dots,g_{k}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\sum_{f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)}\tau\left(\widehat{H}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\,B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{g_{1},\dots,g_{k}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\sum_{f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)}\tau\left(\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{k}}_{g_{k}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdot B^{u}_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\tau\left(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot\Bigl(\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{i-1}}_{g_{i-1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\Bigr)\right)

\displaystyle\approx_{i\cdot\nu^{\prime}_{2}\;}

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}}\tau\left(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot\Bigl(\widehat{G}^{x_{i-1}}_{g_{i-1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\Bigr)\right)

(100)

where the last approximation follows from

Propositions

2.7
and
2.11
and the approximate
commutativity of the
\widehat{G}^{x}
measurements (
Corollary

6.15
).
Continuing, we can use Cauchy-Schwarz to bound (
100
) by

(
100
)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\tau\left(\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\sqrt{\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{i}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\tau\left(\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{i-1}}_{g_{i-1}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{1}}_{g_{1}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\cdot{\bigl(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\bigr)}^{2}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1}\cdot\sqrt{\E_{u,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{i}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\tau\left(\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}\,B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\right)}\;,

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
\widehat{G}^{x}
is projective
and

{\bigl(B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\bigr)}^{2}\leq B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}

for all
u
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\E_{u,x_{i}}\sum_{g_{i}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\tau\left(\widehat{G}^{x_{i}}_{g_{i}}B^{u}_{[f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{u,x}\sum_{a,b:a\neq b}\tau\left(G^{u,x}_{a}B^{u,x}_{b}\right)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}}\E_{u,x}\sum_{a,b:a\neq b}\tau\left(G^{u,x}_{a}A^{u,x}_{b}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\zeta\;.

where the approximation in the second line follows from
Proposition

2.9
and
Eq.

86
.∎

Next we move from
\widehat{H}
to
H
.

Lemma 6.17

(Consistency of
H
with
B
)
.

Let
\nu^{\prime}_{3}=k\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}+k^{2}/n
. Then

H_{[h|_{u}=f]}\simeq_{\nu^{\prime}_{3}}B^{u}_{f}\;.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\E_{u}\sum_{f\neq f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\tau\big(H_{[h\mapsto h|_{u}\mid f^{\prime}]}B^{u}_{f}\big)

(101)

\displaystyle=\E_{u}\sum_{h}\sum_{f\neq h|_{u}}\tau\big(H_{h}B^{u}_{f}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{h}\sum_{f\neq h|_{u}}\tau\big(H^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{h}B^{u}_{f}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{h}\sum_{w:|w|\geq t}\sum_{f\neq h|_{u}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)

(102)

where
g_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{+}
is defined to be
\bot
if
w_{i}=0
, and otherwise
g_{i}=h|_{x_{i}}
.
Because
|w|\geq t
,
there exist at least
t
coordinates
i
such that
g_{i}\neq\bot

and hence
g_{i}=h|_{x_{i}}
. Since
t\geq n-d+1
, using the distance property of
\mathcal{C}
it follows that if
f\neq h|_{u}

then there must exist an
i
such that
g_{i}\neq\bot
and
g_{i}(u)\neq f(x_{i})
.
Thus

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:keep-on-expandin}~=~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{h}\sum_{w:|w|\geq t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f:\exists i:g_{i}\neq\bot,\\
g_{i}(u)\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f:\exists i:g_{i}\neq\bot,\\
g_{i}(u)\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\;.

(103)

Using
Proposition

6.4
(which allows us to switch from sampling
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)
to sampling
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}
independently from
[n]
) and the fact that
\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)
is nonnegative and, when summed over
g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}
and
f
, is at most
1
, we have that
Equation

103

is
(k^{2}/n)
-close to

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f:\exists i:g_{i}\neq\bot,\\
g_{i}(u)\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f:g_{i}\neq\bot,\\
g_{i}(u)\neq f(x_{i})\end{subarray}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)

(by the union bound)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\sum_{b\neq g_{i}(u)}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{[f\mapsto f(x_{i})\mid b]}\big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}

(by
Lemma

6.16
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle k\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}\;.

This establishes the lemma.
∎

Corollary 6.18

(Consistency of
H
with
A
)
.

Let
\nu^{\prime}_{4}=\nu^{\prime}_{3}+\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}
. Then on average over
(u,x)\sim[n]^{m}\times[n]
,

H_{[h\mapsto h(u,x)\mid a]}\simeq_{\nu^{\prime}_{4}}A^{u,x}_{a}\;.

Proof.

Applying
Proposition

2.4
to
Lemma

6.17
we get that

H_{[h\mapsto h(u,x)\mid a]}\simeq_{\nu^{\prime}_{3}}B^{u}_{[f\mapsto f(x)\mid a]}\;.

(104)

Using (
86
) together with
Proposition

2.9
(with measurement “
A^{x}_{a}
” in the Proposition being set to
B^{u}_{[f\mapsto f(x)\mid a]}
, measurement “
B^{x}_{a}
” being set to
A^{x}_{a}
and the submeasurement “
C^{x}_{a}
” being set to
H_{[h\mapsto h(u,x)\mid a]}
) proves the lemma.
∎

6.4.3
Completeness of
H

Definition 6.19
.

Let
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{k}
be a type.
We define the following two subsets of
(\mathcal{C}^{+})^{k}
.

•

We define
\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}
to be the set of tuples
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})
such that
g_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}
for each
i\in\tau
and
g_{i}=\bot
for each
i\notin\tau
.
This is the set of possible outcomes of the measurement
\widehat{H}
of type
\tau
.

•

Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in[n]
.
We define
\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(x)
to be the subset of
\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}
containing only those tuples which are consistent with a global codeword. In other words,
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(x)
iff there exists an
h\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
such that
g_{i}=h|_{x_{i}}
for each
i\in\tau
(recall that
h|_{x_{i}}
denotes the function
h(\cdots,x_{i})\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}
).
Define

\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(x)}=\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}\setminus\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(x).

This contains those tuples of type
\tau
with no consistent global codeword.

Lemma 6.20
.

Let
\nu^{\prime}_{5}=2\frac{k^{2}}{n}+k\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}+\gamma_{m}
. Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\sim[n]
be sampled uniformly at random.
Then

\tau\big(H\big)\,\approx_{\nu^{\prime}_{5}}\,\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\;.

Proof.

By definition,

\displaystyle\tau\big(H\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\;.

(105)

The sum in (
105
)
is over
g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}
which are consistent with a global codeword.
We show that the value of this sum
remains largely unchanged if we drop this condition.
In particular, we claim that

\eqref{eq:sum-restricted-to-global-polynomial}\approx_{\frac{k^{2}}{n}+k\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}+\gamma_{m}}\E_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\;.

(106)

To show this, we note that
\eqref{eq:remove-the-restriction}\geq\eqref{eq:sum-restricted-to-global-polynomial}
. Hence, it suffices to upper bound their difference.

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:remove-the-restriction}-\eqref{eq:sum-restricted-to-global-polynomial}

\displaystyle=\E_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{u}\E_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\;,

(107)

where in the second line we used that
B
is a measurement. Next, we claim that

\eqref{eq:B-appears-out-of-thin-air}\approx_{\frac{k^{2}}{n}+k\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}}\E_{u}\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\hskip 7.97224pt\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\forall i\in\tau,f(y_{i})=g_{i}(u)].

(108)

To show this, we note that
\eqref{eq:B-appears-out-of-thin-air}\geq\eqref{eq:add-in-indicator-for-f-and-g}
.
Thus, it suffices to upper bound their difference
\eqref{eq:B-appears-out-of-thin-air}-\eqref{eq:add-in-indicator-for-f-and-g}
. This is given by

\E_{u}\E_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\exists i\in\tau,f(y_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]\;.

(109)

Recall that
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\sim[n]
are sampled independently and uniformly at random.
Using
Proposition

6.4
,
Equation

109
is
(k^{2}/n)
-close to

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(x)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\exists i\in\tau,f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\exists i\in\tau,f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\exists i\in\tau,f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{i\in\tau}\mathbb{I}[f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)]\Big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\sum_{f:f(x_{i})\neq g_{i}(u)}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\E_{u}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}:g_{i}\neq\bot}\sum_{a\neq g_{i}(u)}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{[f(x_{i})=a]}\big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}

(by
Lemma

6.16
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle k\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}.

Returning to
Equation

108
,
we introduce the notation

\mathsf{Consistent}_{\tau}(g,y,u)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}1&\text{if $\exists f$ such that $f(y_{i})=g_{i}(u)$ for all $i\in\tau$\;,}\\
0&\text{otherwise\;.}\end{array}\right.

Clearly, for any
f
,

\mathbb{I}[\forall i\in\tau,f(y_{i})=g_{i}(u)]\leq\mathbb{I}[\mathsf{Consistent}_{\tau}(g,y,u)]\;.

As a result,

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:add-in-indicator-for-f-and-g}\leq~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\hskip 7.97224pt\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{f}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}B^{u}_{f}\big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\mathsf{Consistent}_{\tau}(g,y,u)]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\hskip 7.97224pt\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\Big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\Big(\sum_{f}B^{u}_{f}\Big)\Big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\mathsf{Consistent}_{\tau}(g,y,u)]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\E_{u}\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\hskip 7.97224pt\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\cdot\mathbb{I}[\mathsf{Consistent}_{\tau}(g,y,u)]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\hskip 7.97224pt\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\cdot\Pr_{u}\Big(\mathsf{Consistent}_{\tau}(g,y,u)\Big).

(110)

Let us now fix
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\in\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)
,
a type
\tau
such that
|\tau|\geq t
,
and
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}
.
Suppose without loss of generality that
\tau_{1}=\cdots=\tau_{t}=1
,
so that
g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes m}
.
Then by
Proposition

2.20
there is a unique
h^{*}\in\mathcal{C}^{\otimes(m+1)}
which interpolates
g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}
.
In other words, for all
1\leq i\leq t
and for all
u\in[n]^{m}
,

h^{*}(u,y_{i})=g_{i}(u).

On the other hand, because
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}
,
there exists an
i^{*}\in\tau
such that
g_{i^{*}}\neq(h^{*})|_{y_{i^{*}}}
.
Hence,

\Pr_{u}\Big(\mathsf{Consistent}_{\tau}(g,y,u)\Big)\leq\Pr_{u}\Big(g_{i^{*}}(u)=h^{*}(u,y_{i^{*}})\Big)=\Pr_{u}\Big(g_{i^{*}}(u)=(h^{*})|_{y_{i^{*}}}(u)\Big)\leq\Big(1-\frac{d^{m}}{n^{m}}\Big)\,=\,\gamma_{m}\;,

by Proposition
2.18
.
As a result,

(
110
)

\displaystyle\leq\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\hskip 7.97224pt\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\overline{\mathsf{Global}_{\tau}(y)}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\cdot\gamma_{m}

\displaystyle\leq\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\hskip 7.97224pt\sim\mathsf{distinct}([n],k)\end{subarray}}\sum_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\cdot\gamma_{m}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{m}\;.

This establishes
Equation

106
.
Finally,
Equation

106
is
(k^{2}/n)
-close to

\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)\;,

as desired.
∎

Lemma 6.21
.

Recall the error parameter
\nu^{\prime}_{2}
from
Corollary

6.15
. Let
\nu^{\prime}_{6}=2k^{2}\nu^{\prime}_{2}
.
Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\sim[n]
be sampled uniformly at random.
Then

\E_{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\dots,g_{k}}\big)\approx_{\nu^{\prime}_{6}}\sum_{i=t}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\tau\big(G^{i}(\mathbf{1}-G)^{k-i}\big)\;.

Proof.

We begin by introducing some notation that we will use throughout the proof.
Let
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{k}
be a type.
Then we define

\tau_{<\ell}=(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{\ell-1})\in\{0,1\}^{\ell-1}\;,\quad\tau_{>\ell}=(\tau_{\ell+1},\ldots,\tau_{k})\in\{0,1\}^{k-\ell}\;,

and we define
\tau_{\leq\ell}
and
\tau_{\geq\ell}
similarly.
In addition, given
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in(\mathcal{C}^{+})^{k}
, we define

g_{<\ell}=(g_{1},\ldots,g_{\ell-1})\;,\qquad g_{>\ell}=(g_{\ell+1},\ldots,g_{k})

and we define
g_{\leq\ell}
and
g_{\geq\ell}
similarly.
Using this notation, we can write

\widehat{H}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}}=\widehat{H}^{x_{\ell},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{\ell},\ldots,g_{k}}\;.

Next, we introduce the notation

\widehat{G}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}}=\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\cdots\widehat{G}^{x_{k}}_{g_{k}}\;.

This satisfies the recurrence relation

\widehat{G}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}}=\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\;.

(111)

Furthermore, we can write

\widehat{H}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}}=(\widehat{G}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}})\cdot(\widehat{G}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}})^{\dagger}.

(112)

To prove the lemma we show that for each
1\leq\ell\leq k
,

\displaystyle\E_{x_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{g_{\geq\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}}\cdot(G^{|\tau_{<\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{(\ell-1)-|\tau_{<\ell}|})\big)

\displaystyle\approx_{2k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}~

\displaystyle\E_{x_{>\ell}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{g_{>\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{>\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot(G^{|\tau_{\leq\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{\ell-|\tau_{\leq\ell}|})\big)\;.

(113)

If we then repeatedly apply
Equation

113
for
\ell=1,\ldots,k
,
we derive

\displaystyle\E_{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\dots,g_{k}}\big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\E_{x_{\geq 1}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{g_{\geq 1}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{\geq 1}}_{g_{\geq 1}}\big)

\displaystyle\approx_{2k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}~

\displaystyle\E_{x_{\geq 2}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{g_{\geq 2}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq 2}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{\geq 2}}_{g_{\geq 2}}\cdot(G^{|\tau_{\leq 1}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-|\tau_{\leq 1}|})\big)

\displaystyle\approx_{2k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}~

\displaystyle\E_{x_{\geq 3}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq d+1}\sum_{g_{\geq 3}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq 3}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{\geq 3}}_{g_{\geq 3}}\cdot(G^{|\tau_{\leq 2}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{2-|\tau_{\leq 2}|})\big)

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle\approx_{2k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}~

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\tau\big(G^{|\tau|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{k-|\tau|}\big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=t}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\tau\big(G^{i}(\mathbf{1}-G)^{k-i}\big)\;.

This proves the lemma.

We now prove
Equation

113
.
To begin, for each
1\leq\ell\leq k+1
and
\tau_{\geq\ell}\in\{0,1\}^{k-\ell+1}
,
we define the operator

S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}=\sum_{\tau_{<\ell}:|\tau|\geq t}G^{|\tau_{<\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{(\ell-1)-|\tau_{<\ell}|}\;.

(114)

Then the statement in
Equation

113
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\E_{x_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{g_{\geq\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{\geq\ell}}_{g_{\geq\ell}}\cdot S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\big)

\displaystyle\approx_{2k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}~

\displaystyle\E_{x_{>\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{>\ell}}\sum_{g_{>\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{>\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot S_{\tau_{>\ell}}\big)\;.

(115)

To prove this, we will use several facts about
S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}
.
First,
S
is self-adjoint and positive semidefinite.
This is because each term in
Equation

114

is a product of
G
and
(\mathbf{1}-G)
.
These operators commute with each other,
and both are self-adjoint and positive semidefinite.
Next,
S
is bounded:

\displaystyle S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau_{<\ell}:|\tau|\geq t}G^{|\tau_{<\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{(\ell-1)-|\tau_{<\ell}|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau_{<\ell}}G^{|\tau_{<\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{(\ell-1)-|\tau_{<\ell}|}

\displaystyle=(G+(\mathbf{1}-G))^{\ell-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\;.

(116)

In addition, for any
\tau_{\ell}\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\E_{x_{\ell}}\sum_{g_{\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\ell}}}\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}G&\text{if }\tau_{\ell}=1\;,\\
(\mathbf{1}-G)&\text{if }\tau_{\ell}=0\;,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}}\;.

(119)

Thus, for any
\tau_{>\ell}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau_{\ell}}S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\cdot\Big(\E_{x_{\ell}}\sum_{g_{\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\ell}}}\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau_{\ell}}S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\cdot(G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau_{\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{<\ell}:|\tau|\geq t}G^{|\tau_{<\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{(\ell-1)-|\tau_{<\ell}|}\cdot(G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau_{\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{<\ell}:|\tau|\geq t}G^{|\tau_{\leq\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{\ell-|\tau_{\leq\ell}|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau_{\leq\ell}:|\tau|\geq t}G^{|\tau_{\leq\ell}|}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{\ell-|\tau_{\leq\ell}|}

\displaystyle=S_{\tau_{>\ell}}\;.

(120)

Finally, for any
\tau_{\geq\ell}
,

\displaystyle S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\cdot\Big(\E_{x_{\ell}}\sum_{g_{\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\ell}}}\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\Big)\cdot S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\cdot(G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}})\cdot S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}

(by
Equation

119
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sqrt{G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}}}\cdot(S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}})^{2}\cdot\sqrt{G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}}}

(because
S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}
commutes with
G
and
(\mathbf{1}-G)
)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sqrt{G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}}}\cdot I\cdot\sqrt{G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}}}

(by
Equation

116
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle G^{\tau_{\ell}}\cdot(\mathbf{1}-G)^{1-\tau_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\Big(\E_{x_{\ell}}\sum_{g_{\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\ell}}}\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\Big)\;,

(121)

where the last step uses
Equation

119
again.
This concludes the set of facts we will need about
S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}
.

Now we prove
Equation

115
.
To start, we write using the definition

\displaystyle\E_{x_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{(g_{\ell},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{\ell},\dots,x_{k}}_{g_{\ell},\dots,g_{k}}\cdot S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\E_{x_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{(g_{\ell},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\cdot(\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot(\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}})^{\dagger})\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\cdot S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\big)\;.

(122)

Next, we commute the leftmost
\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}
to the right. Using
Corollary

6.15
and
Proposition

2.11
we get

\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\approx_{k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}

and similarly

\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\cdot(\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}})^{\dagger}\approx_{k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}(\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}})^{\dagger}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\;.

Combining with
Proposition

2.7
and injecting back into (
122
) we get

\E_{x_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{(g_{\ell},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{\ell},\dots,x_{k}}_{g_{\ell},\dots,g_{k}}\cdot S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\big)\approx_{2k\nu^{\prime}_{2}}\E_{x_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{(g_{\ell},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}}\tau\big((\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot(\widehat{G}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}})^{\dagger})\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\cdot S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\big)\;.

(123)

We end by noting that

(
123
)

\displaystyle=\E_{x_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\sum_{(g_{\ell},g_{>\ell})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot(S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\cdot\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}})\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x_{>\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{>\ell}}\sum_{g_{>\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{>\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot\Big(\sum_{\tau_{\ell}}S_{\tau_{\geq\ell}}\cdot\Big(\E_{x_{\ell}}\sum_{g_{\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{\ell}}}\widehat{G}^{x_{\ell}}_{g_{\ell}}\Big)\Big)\big)

\displaystyle=\E_{x_{>\ell}}\sum_{\tau_{>\ell}}\sum_{g_{>\ell}\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau_{>\ell}}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{>\ell}}_{g_{>\ell}}\cdot S_{\tau_{>\ell}}\big)\;.

(by
Equation

120
)

This concludes the proof of

Equation

115

and therefore proves the lemma.
∎

The following lemma follows from the functional calculus for bounded self-adjoint operators. In the lemma,
\mathscr{A}
may be any von Neumann subalgebra of
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{H})
and
\tau
a normal tracial state on
\mathscr{A}
, but we state (and use it) for the algebra and tracial state associated with the strategy
\mathscr{S}
.

Lemma 6.22
.

Let
0<\theta<1
and let
k,t>0
be integers such that
k\geq 2t/\theta
.
Define the function
F:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

F(x)=\sum_{r=t}^{k}\binom{k}{r}x^{r}(1-x)^{k-r}.

Then for any self-adjoint
X\in\mathscr{A}
such that
0\leq X\leq\mathbf{1}
and
\tau\big(X\big)\geq 1-\kappa
, it holds that
F(X)\in\mathscr{A}
and moreover

\tau\big(F(X)\big)\geq 1-\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}-e^{-\theta^{2}k/2}.

Proof.

The statement that
F(X)\in\mathscr{A}
follows from the functional calculus for bounded self-adjoint operators.
Let
\mu
be the projection-valued measure on the Borel
\sigma
-algebra in
[0,1]
associated to
X
by the spectral theorem for bounded self-adjoint operators (see e.g.
[
Hal13
, Theorem 7.12]
). Then
X=\int_{[0,1]}\lambda\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)
, and using that
\tau
is normal, there exists a trace class operator
A\in\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{H})
such that

\displaystyle\tau(X)

\displaystyle=\mbox{\rm tr}\Big(\int_{[0,1]}\lambda\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)A\Big)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]}\lambda\,\mbox{\rm tr}\big(A\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]}\lambda\,\mathrm{d}\nu(\lambda)\;,

where
\nu
is the real-valued measure on
[0,1]
defined by
\,\mathrm{d}\nu(E)=\int_{E}\mbox{\rm tr}(A\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda))
.
Thus the
assumption
\tau(X)\geq 1-\kappa
implies that

\int_{\lambda}\lambda\,\mathrm{d}\nu(\lambda)\geq 1-\kappa
.
By Markov’s inequality, for any
0<\theta<1
,

\int_{[0,1]}\mathbb{I}[1-\lambda\geq 1-\theta]\,\mathrm{d}\nu(\lambda)\,\leq\,\frac{\int_{[0,1]}(1-\lambda)\,\mathrm{d}\nu}{1-\theta}\,\leq\,\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}\;.

(124)

We now evaluate
\tau(F(X))
.
Observe that for
0\leq p\leq 1
,
F(p)
is precisely the
probability of observing at least
t
successes out of
k

i.i.d. Bernoulli trials, each of which succeeds with probability

p
.
In other words, it is the probability that
Y:=Y_{1}+\cdots+Y_{k}\geq t
,
where
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k}\sim\mathrm{Bernoulli}(p)
.
We can bound this probability by the additive Chernoff bound (see the second additive bound in
[
Blu11
]
):

\displaystyle\Pr(Y<t)

\displaystyle=\Pr(Y<pk-(pk-t))

\displaystyle=\Pr\Big(Y<pk-\Big(p-\frac{t}{k}\Big)\cdot k\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-2\Big(p-\frac{t}{k}\Big)^{2}\cdot k\Big)\;.

Thus,

F(p)=\Pr(Y\geq t)=1-\Pr(Y<t)\geq 1-\exp\Big(-2\Big(p-\frac{t}{k}\Big)^{2}\cdot k\Big)\;.

(125)

Putting the pieces together, we compute
\tau(F(X))
:

\displaystyle\tau\big(F(X)\big)

\displaystyle=\tau\Big(\int_{[0,1]}F(\lambda)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)\Big)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]}F(\lambda)\,\tau\big(A\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{[0,1]}F(\lambda)\,\mathbb{I}[\lambda\geq\theta]\,\mathrm{d}\nu(\lambda)

(
F
is nonnegative)

\displaystyle\geq F(\theta)\,\int_{[0,1]}\mathbb{I}[\lambda\geq\theta]\,\mathrm{d}\nu(\lambda)

(
F
is non-decreasing)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}\Big)\cdot F(\theta)

(by
Equation

124
)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}\Big)\cdot\Big(1-\exp\Big(-2\Big(\theta-\frac{t}{k}\Big)^{2}\cdot k\Big)\Big)

(
Equation

125
)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}-\exp\Big(-2\Big(\theta-\frac{d}{k}\Big)^{2}\cdot k\Big)\;.

(126)

Finally, we note that because
k\geq 2t/\theta
,
we have
\theta/2\geq t/k
.
This implies that

\theta-t/k\geq\theta-\theta/2=\theta/2
,
and so

(\theta-t/k)^{2}\geq(\theta/2)^{2}=\theta^{2}/4
.
As a result,

\exp\Big(2\Big(\theta-\frac{t}{k}\Big)^{2}\cdot k\Big)\geq\exp\Big(\theta^{2}k/2\Big)\;.

Equivalently,

\exp\Big(-2\Big(\theta-\frac{t}{k}\Big)^{2}\cdot k\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\theta^{2}k/2\Big)\;.

Thus, we conclude

\tau\big(F(X)\big)\geq 1-\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}-\exp\Big(-\theta^{2}k/2\Big)\;.\qed

Corollary 6.23

(Completeness of
H
)
.

Recall the error parameters
\nu_{5}^{\prime},\nu_{6}^{\prime}
from
Lemma

6.20
and
Lemma

6.21
, respectively. Let
\nu=\nu_{5}^{\prime}+\nu_{6}^{\prime}
and let
k\geq 12mt
be an integer. Then
\nu
is equal to some polynomial function
\mathrm{poly}(m,t,k)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1})
and furthermore

\tau\big(H\big)\geq 1-\kappa\cdot\left(1+\frac{1}{3m}\right)-\nu-e^{-\frac{k}{72m^{2}}}\;.

Proof.

We begin by approximating the completeness as follows.

\displaystyle\tau\big(H\big)

\displaystyle\approx_{\nu^{\prime}_{5}}\E_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}\sum_{\tau:|\tau|\geq t}\sum_{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})\in\mathsf{Outcomes}_{\tau}}\tau\big(\widehat{H}^{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}_{g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}}\big)

(by
Lemma

6.20
)

\displaystyle\approx_{\nu^{\prime}_{6}}\sum_{i=d+1}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\tau\big(G^{i}(\mathbf{1}-G)^{k-i}\big)

(by
Lemma

6.21
)

\displaystyle=\tau\big(F(G)\big)\

where
F
is the function from
Lemma

6.22
. We unroll the error parameters in terms of
\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,m,n,t,k
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{m}=1-\frac{d^{m}}{n^{m}}\leq\frac{mt}{n}

(
Proposition

2.18
)

\displaystyle\nu_{1}=8(\sqrt{\zeta}+\sqrt{(m+1)\delta})

(
Lemma

6.2
)

\displaystyle\nu_{2}=4(\gamma_{m}+\nu_{1})

(
Lemma

6.3
)

\displaystyle\nu_{2}^{\prime}=27\nu_{2}^{1/4}

(
Corollary

6.15
)

\displaystyle\nu_{3}^{\prime}=k\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}+k^{2}/n

(
Lemma

6.17
)

\displaystyle\nu_{4}^{\prime}=\nu^{\prime}_{3}+\sqrt{2(m+1)\varepsilon}

(
Corollary

6.18
)

\displaystyle\nu_{5}^{\prime}=2\frac{k^{2}}{n}+k\nu^{\prime\prime}_{3}+\gamma_{m}

(
Lemma

6.20
)

\displaystyle\nu_{6}^{\prime}=2k^{2}\nu^{\prime}_{2}

(
Lemma

6.21
)

One can see that each of the error parameters
\gamma_{m},\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{6}^{\prime}
can be expressed in the form
\mathrm{poly}(m,t,k)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1})
where each error parameter is a different polynomial function. Thus letting
\nu=\nu_{5}^{\prime}+\nu_{6}^{\prime}=\mathrm{poly}(m,t,k)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\varepsilon,\delta,\zeta,n^{-1})
, we have that

\tau(H)\geq\tau(F(G))-\nu~.

Define
\theta=\frac{1}{6m}
. Note that
k\geq 2t/\theta
by assumption, and that
\frac{1}{1-\theta}\leq 1+\frac{1}{3m}
.
As a result,
k
and
\theta
satisfy the hypotheses of
Lemma

6.22
, which implies

\displaystyle\tau\big(F(G)\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}-e^{-\theta^{2}k/2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\kappa}{1-\theta}-e^{-\frac{k}{72m^{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\kappa\cdot\left(1+\frac{1}{3m}\right)-e^{-\frac{k}{72m^{2}}}.

In total, we have

\tau\big(H\big)\geq 1-\kappa\cdot\left(1+\frac{1}{3m}\right)-\nu-e^{-\frac{k}{72m^{2}}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

We conclude by observing that the conclusion of
Lemma

4.3
follows immediately by letting
k
denote the parameter
r
in the statement of
Lemma

4.3

10
10

10

We use
r
in the statement of
Lemma

4.3
in order to avoid confusion with the dimension of the code
\mathcal{C}
, which is also denoted by
k
.
, combining the bounds from
Corollary

6.18
, and
Corollary

6.23
using the same “completion” procedure as described in Section
6.3.3
.

Appendix A
Operators on expander graphs

Let
G
be an
n
-vertex graph with some distribution on the edges, such that the marginal distribution on the vertices is uniform. Let

K=\E_{(u,v)\sim G}|u\rangle\!\langle v|

and let

L=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{1}-K

denote the normalized Laplacian of
G
.

Proposition A.1
.

L=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\E_{(u,v)\sim G}({|u\rangle}-{|v\rangle})({\langle u|}-{\langle v|})
.

Thus
L
is positive semidefinite and has
0
-eigenvector

{|\varphi_{0}\rangle}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{u\in V(G)}{|u\rangle}

where
V(G)
denotes the vertex set of
G
.

Let
0=\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}\leq\cdots\leq\lambda_{n}
denote the eigenvalues of
L
in non-decreasing order. Then we have that

L\geq\lambda_{2}\,(\mathbf{1}-|\varphi_{0}\rangle\!\langle\varphi_{0}|).

(127)

The following lemma is a variant of the familiar Poincaré inequality for expander graphs. It states that “global” averages of an operator-valued function on an expander graph can be approximated by averages of the function on random edges of the graph.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a Hilbert space and let
\rho
be a positive linear functional
on
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{H})
.
Let
\{A^{u}\}_{u\in[n]}
be bounded operators on
\mathcal{H}
.
Suppose
G
is an
n
-vertex graph associated with an edge distribution that
has the uniform marginal distribution on the vertices.
Then

\E_{u,v\sim[n]}\,\rho((A^{u}-A^{v})^{*}(A^{u}-A^{v}))\leq\frac{1}{n\lambda_{2}}\E_{(u,v)\sim G}\,\rho((A^{u}-A^{v})^{*}(A^{u}-A^{v}))

where the expectation on the left hand side is over uniformly random vertices
u,v\sim[n]
.

Proof.

Define the following map
V:\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{C}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{H}
:

V=\sum_{u}{|u\rangle}\otimes A^{u}.

The proof is based on estimating the Rayleigh quotient

\frac{V^{*}(L\otimes\mathbf{1})V}{V^{*}V}\;.

Observe that

V^{*}(L\otimes\mathbf{1})V=\frac{1}{2}\E_{(u,v)\sim G}(A^{u}-A^{v})^{*}(A^{u}-A^{v}).

(128)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle V^{*}(L\otimes\mathbf{1})V

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{2}\cdot V^{*}\Big(\mathbf{1}-|\varphi_{0}\rangle\!\langle\varphi_{0}|\Big)V

(129)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{2}\cdot\Big(\sum_{u}(A^{u})^{*}A^{u}-\big(\sum_{v}n^{-1/2}A^{v}\big)^{*}\big(\sum_{v}n^{-1/2}A^{v}\big)\Big)

(130)

\displaystyle=n\lambda_{2}\cdot\Big(\E_{u\sim[n]}(A^{u})^{*}A^{u}-\overline{A}^{*}\overline{A}\Big)

(131)

\displaystyle=n\lambda_{2}\cdot\Big(\E_{u\sim[n]}(A^{u})^{*}(A^{u}-\overline{A})\Big)

(132)

\displaystyle=\frac{n\lambda_{2}}{2}\cdot\Big(\E_{u,v\sim[n]}(A^{u}-A^{v})^{*}(A^{u}-A^{v})\Big)\;.

(133)

where in the second line we computed
V^{*}V=\sum_{u}(A^{u})^{*}A^{u}
and
V^{*}{|\varphi_{0}\rangle}=\sum_{u}n^{-1/2}(A^{u})^{*}
, and in the third line we defined
\overline{A}=\E_{v\sim[n]}A^{v}
. Applying the positive linear functional
\rho
on
Equation

128
and
Equation

133
yields the statement of the Lemma.
∎
